### Zero lever (antenna Z), second pass

1904 runs; every scheme gives identical results in 1904 of them (the lever schemes only act on an antenna off the origin). With GNSS and IMU only in h300 to h1800 the zero-lever reference has 321 tz, 1001 heading>0.5 and 7 xy>1 in 1400 runs.

### Selection (the pre-declared criteria of experiments/eskf_lever.cpp)

GNSS-and-IMU-only rows with a lever arm (antennas A, B, M) in the suites h300, h600, h900, h1800: 2448 runs per scheme in the envelope cells (stand, line, startstop, turn at gyro bias <= 0.2 deg/s, every duration), 1752 runs per scheme in the other cells (drive, spin, 0.5 deg/s). The zero-lever reference (antenna Z) has 1400 runs, of which 321 have tz.

The zero-lever reference itself has tz or circ in 178 of its 816 runs in the envelope cells (the accelerometer bias of 0.3 m/s^2 and the 120 s dropouts, from which the plain update does not recover either): E > 0 for a lever-arm scheme is then not by itself a defect of the scheme, and E' counts the runs that the zero-lever row of the same data survives.

**None of the candidates has E = 0.** The declared order is then applied to all of them with E as its first key (fewest runs with tz or circ in the envelope cells): S7a35r05 (E 451, X 30, C 0, H 0, median max xy 0.135 m).
Order of all candidates: S7a35r05 (E 451, X 30, C 0, H 0, 0.135 m), S7a35 (E 454, X 35, C 0, H 0, 0.143 m), S7a50 (E 489, X 55, C 0, H 1, 0.195 m), S7a35r20 (E 502, X 89, C 0, H 0, 0.170 m), S7r05 (E 931, X 51, C 0, H 1, 0.253 m), S7a (E 942, X 67, C 0, H 1, 0.259 m), S7 (E 1071, X 274, C 0, H 0, 0.306 m), S7r20 (E 1072, X 129, C 0, H 1, 0.304 m).

**Shipped: S7a35, not S7a35r05.** The declared order puts S7a35r05 first by 3 runs in E (451 against 454, of 2448 runs per scheme in the envelope cells) and 5 in X (30 against 35): inside what two seeds per configuration can tell apart. The suite hvib, written after that order was declared and run after the first results, shows that the release at the lower threshold opens on a robot that does not accelerate once the accelerometer noise is 0.1 m/s^2 per axis (table "Accelerometer noise" below), and the threshold the order prefers is the less robust one. This is a deviation from the declared order, and it is the only one.

### Accelerometer noise (suite hvib: 300 s, standing and straight line at 1.5 m/s, gyro bias 0.1 deg/s, accelerometer bias 0.1 m/s^2; lever-arm rows)

The filter is told the noise of the simulated IMU. "release open" is the share of lever updates with the release open: on a robot that does not accelerate it is a false release (the heading is then corrected from data that does not show it).

# Lever-arm study, third pass: standing, then moving

Suites hsg (the schemes S8r, S8a and S8 were developed on these runs) and hsgv (the same configurations on other data, run once after the schemes were fixed). Each run stands for 300, 600, 900 or 1,800 s at the origin with a gyro bias of 0 to 0.2 deg/s per axis, then starts (sgstart: 0 to 1.5 m/s in 3 s, then the start-stop cycle), drives (sgdrive: the same start, then a circle of radius 6 m at 1.5 m/s) or turns on the spot (sgturn: 0.3 rad/s for 4 s of every 5 s) for 160 s. GNSS and IMU only; antennas A, B, M and the zero lever Z; 4 seeds. tz = tilt > 0.05 rad or abs z > 0.5 m at an epoch from 10 s on (the standing time counts); "excess" = the lever-arm run has tz and the zero-lever run of the same data has not; "z>1" = abs z > 1 m; "tilt>0.3" = tilt > 0.3 rad. The bar (declared in eskf_lever.cpp): in each motion, the lever-arm rows have no more tz than the zero-lever rows of the same data (pairs counted: tz of the lever run <= tz of its zero-lever run, summed), and no run with z>1 or tilt>0.3 in the envelope motions (sgstart, sgdrive with an antenna that has a horizontal arm: A, B).
